<div class='question question'>

Work out $2394 \div 9$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2394 \div 9 \\\\
&= \dfrac{2394}{9} \\\\
&= \dfrac{266 \times 9}{9} \\\\
&= \dfrac{266 \times \cancel 9}{\cancel 9} \\\\
&= 266
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$266$

</div>
</div>

$
</code></pre>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Work out $\dfrac{3}{5}$ of $45$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&=  \dfrac {3} {5} \ \text {of} \ 45 \\\\
&=  \dfrac {3} {5} \times 45 \\\\
&=  \dfrac {3 \times 45} {5} \\\\
&=  \dfrac {3 \times 9 \times 5} {5} \\\\
&=  \dfrac {3 \times 9 \times \cancel {5}} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
&=  3 \times 9 \\\\
&=  27
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$27$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&=  \dfrac {3} {5} \ \text {of} \ 45 \\\\
&=  \dfrac {3} {5} \times 45 \\\\
&=  \dfrac {3 \times 45} {5} \\\\
&=  \dfrac {3 \times 9 \times 5} {5} \\\\
&=  \dfrac {3 \times 9 \times \cancel {5}} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
&=  3 \times 9 \\\\
&=  27
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the cost of one Doggy Chew?

Give your answer in pence.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 1 packet}          &= \pounds \dfrac {3.43} {7} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {343} {7} \ \text {pence} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {49 \times 7} {7} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {49 \times \cancel {7}} {\cancel {7}} \\\\
                                 &= 49 \ \text {pence}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$49 \ \text {pence}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 1 packet}          &= \pounds \dfrac {3.43} {7} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {343} {7} \ \text {pence} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {49 \times 7} {7} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {49 \times \cancel {7}} {\cancel {7}} \\\\
                                 &= 49 \ \text {pence}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$49 \ \text {pence}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Charolette buys $2$ packets of Doggy Chews and pays with a $\pounds 20$ note.

How much change should she receive?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 2 packets}      &= 3.43 \times 2 \\\\
                              &= \pounds 6.86 \\\\
\text {Change}                &= 20 - 6.86 \\\\
                              &= \pounds 13.14
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 13.14$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}

                              &= \pounds 6.86 \\\\
\text {Change}                &= 20 - 6.86 \\\\
                              &= \pounds 13.14
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 13.14$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$4 \times \boxed{\phantom{1}} + 8 = 44$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
4 \times a + 8  &= 44 \\\\
4 a             &= 44 - 8 \\\\
4 a             &= 36 \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {36} {4} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {9 \times 4} {4} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel {4}} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
a               &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$

\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

$3 \times (5 - \boxed{\phantom{1}}) = 12$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
3 \times (5 - a)    &= 12 \\\\
15 - 3 a            &= 12 \\\\
3 a                 &= 15 - 12 \\\\
3 a                 &= 3 \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {3} {3} \\\\
a                   &= 1
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1$

<div class='workings'>

a                 &= \dfrac {4 \times 2 \times 5 \times 2} {4 \times 2 \times 2} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {\cancel {4} \times \cancel {2} \times 5 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {4} \times \cancel {2} \times \cancel {2}} \\\\
a                 &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

Which is larger, $75\%$ or $\dfrac{5}{8}$?

Give a reason for your answer.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 75\% or \dfrac{5}{8} \\\\
&= \dfrac{75}{100} or \dfrac{5}{8} \\\\
&= \dfrac{25 \times 3}{25 \times 4} or \dfrac{5}{8} \\\\
&= \dfrac{\cancel{25} \times 3}{\cancel{25} \times 4} or \dfrac{5}{8} \\\\
&= \dfrac{3}{4} is greater than \dfrac{5}{8}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$75\%$

</div>
</div>

&= \dfrac{3}{4} is greater than \dfrac{5}{8}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

The number machine below adds $3$ to the input number and then multiplies by $2$.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-14-subquestion-1-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-14-subquestion-1-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-14-subquestion-1-00.png")

Complete the input and output table for this machine.

| **input** | **output** |
|:---------:|:----------:|
|    $1$    |     $8$    |
|    $0$    |  $\ldots$  |
|  $\ldots$ |    $14$    |

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

|                  **input**                                                                              |                  **output**                                                                                |
|:-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------:|:----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------:|
|                      $1$                                                                                |                    $8$                                                                                     |
|                      $0$                                                                                |  $\green{(0 + 3) \times 2 = 3 \times 2 = 6} \ \maroonC{\footnotesize{(1)}}$  |
|  $\green{\dfrac {14} {2} - 3 = 7 - 3 = 4} \ \maroonC{\footnotesize{(2)}}$ |                    $14$                                                                                    |

Completed table:

| **input** | **output** |
|:---------:|:----------:|
|    $1$    |     $8$    |
|    $0$    |  $6$       |
|  $4$      |    $14$    |

</div>
</div>

|  $\rtq_katex_color_answer{\dfrac {14} {2} - 3 = 7 - 3 = 4} \ \rtq_katex_color_info{\footnotesize{(2)}}$ |                    $14$                                                                                    |

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A different number machine produces the input and output table below 

| **input** | **output** |
|:---------:|:----------:|
|    $0$    |     $3$    |
|    $1$    |     $5$    |
|    $2$    |     $7$    |

What is the rule of this number machine?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$(\text{Input} \times 2) + 3 = \text{Output}$

Let's verify.

| **input** |            **output**               |
|:---------:|:-----------------------------------:|
|    $0$    |     $0 \times 2 + 3 = 0 + 3 = 3$    |
|    $1$    |     $1 \times 2 + 3 = 2 + 3 = 5$    |
|    $2$    |     $2 \times 2 + 3 = 4 + 3 = 7$    |

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$(\text{Input} \times 2) + 3 = \text{Output}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$(\text{Input} \times 2) + 3 = \text{Output}$
<emptyline>

|    $1$    |     $1 \times 2 + 3 = 2 + 3 = 5$    |
|    $2$    |     $2 \times 2 + 3 = 4 + 3 = 7$    |
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<div class='answercodeblock'>

<div class='question question'>

Mabel found her grandmother's recipe to make scones. 

Here are the ingredients to make $12$ scones:

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-15-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-15-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-15-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>

</code></pre>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If Mabel wants to make $18$ scones, 

How many grams of self raising flour will she need?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of flour needed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
12 \ \text{scones} : 200 \ \text{grams}             &= 18 \ \text{scones} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac{12} {200}                                &= \dfrac {18} {a} \\\\
12 \times a                                     &= 18 \times 200 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {18 \times 200} {12} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times 6 \times 2 \times 100} {2 \times 6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel 6 \times \cancel 2 \times 100} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 6} \\\\
a                                               &= 3 \times 100 \\\\
a                                               &= 300 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Flour needed $= 300 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{scones}                           &:& \text{flour} \\\\
= & 12                                      &:& 200 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {200} {12} \\\\
= & 1 \times 18                             &:& \dfrac {200 \times 18} {12} \\\\
= & 18                                      &:& \dfrac {2 \times 100 \times 6 \times 3} {6 \times 2} \\\\
= & 18                                      &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 100 \times \cancel 6 \times 3} {\cancel 6 \times \cancel 2} \\\\
= & 18                                      &:& 100 \times 3 \\\\
= & 18                                      &:& 300 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Flour needed $= 300 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$300 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the amount of flour needed $= a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
12 \ \text{scones} : 200 \ \text{grams}             &= 18 \ \text{scones} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac{12} {200}                                &= \dfrac {18} {a} \\\\
12 \times a                                     &= 18 \times 200 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {18 \times 200} {12} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times 6 \times 2 \times 100} {2 \times 6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel 6 \times \cancel 2 \times 100} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 6} \\\\
a                                               &= 3 \times 100 \\\\
a                                               &= 300 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Flour needed $= 300 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>

= & 18                                      &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 100 \times \cancel 6 \times 3} {\cancel 6 \times \cancel 2} \\\\

<emptyline>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many grams of caster sugar will she need?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of caster sugar needed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
12 \ \text{scones} : 30 \ \text{grams}              &= 18 \ \text{scones} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac{12} {30}                                 &= \dfrac {18} {a} \\\\
12 \times a                                     &= 18 \times 30 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {18 \times 30} {12} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times 6 \times 2 \times 15} {2 \times 6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel 6 \times \cancel 2 \times 15} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 6} \\\\
a                                               &= 3 \times 15 \\\\
a                                               &= 45 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Caster sugar needed $= 45 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{scones}                           &:& \text{caster sugar} \\\\
= & 12                                      &:& 30 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {30} {12} \\\\
= & 1 \times 18                             &:& \dfrac {30 \times 18} {12} \\\\
= & 18                                      &:& \dfrac {2 \times 15 \times 6 \times 3} {6 \times 2} \\\\
= & 18                                      &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 15 \times \cancel 6 \times 3} {\cancel 6 \times \cancel 2} \\\\
= & 18                                      &:& 15 \times 3 \\\\
= & 18                                      &:& 45 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Caster sugar needed $= 45 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Mabel only has $1.5 \ \text{kg}$ of flour, but has plenty of all the other ingredients.

What is the largest number of scones she can make?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the largest number of scones she can make $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
200 \ \text{g} : 12 \ \text{scones}             &= 1.5 \ \text{kg} : a \ \text{scones} \\\\
\dfrac{200 \ \text{g}} {12}                     &= \dfrac {1.5 \ \text{kg}} {a} \\\\
\dfrac{200 \ \text{g}} {12}                     &= \dfrac {1500 \ \text{g}} {a} \\\\
200 \times a                                    &= 1500 \times 12 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {1500 \times 12} {200} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {100 \times 15 \times 2 \times 6} {2 \times 100} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {100} \times 15 \times \cancel 2 \times 6} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel {100}} \\\\
a                                               &= 15 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= 90
\end{aligned}
$

Maximum scones $= 90$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{flour}                            &:& \text{scones} \\\\
= & 200 \ \text{g}                          &:& 12 \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{g}                            &:& \dfrac {12} {200} \\\\
= & 1 \times 1500 \ \text{g}                &:& \dfrac {12 \times 1500} {200} \\\\
= & 1.5 \ \text{kg}                         &:& \dfrac {12 \times 1500} {200} \\\\
= & 1.5                                     &:& \dfrac {12 \times 1500} {200} \\\\
= & 1.5                                     &:& \dfrac {2 \times 6 \times 100 \times 15} {2 \times 100} \\\\
= & 1.5                                     &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 6 \times \cancel {100} \times 15} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel {100}} \\\\
= & 1.5                                     &:& 6 \times 15 \\\\
= & 1.5 \ \text{kg}                         &:& 90
\end{array}
$

Maximum scones $= 90$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$90$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {100} \times 15 \times \cancel 2 \times 6} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel {100}} \\\\
a                                               &= 15 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= 90
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Maximum scones $= 90$
</code></pre>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Jennie and Freddie have each thought of a number.

The difference between their numbers is $8$.

The sum of their numbers is $30$.

What are the two numbers?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number Jennie thought of $= a$

Let the number Freddie thought of $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a - b      &= 8 \\\\
a + b      &= 30
\end{aligned}
$

We have got $2$ variables and we have $2$ equations to solve them. Let's eliminate to reduce it to just one variable. 

Let's add above two equation.

$
\begin{aligned}
a - b + a + b   &= 8 + 30 \\\\
2a              &= 38 \\\\
a               &= \dfrac{38}{2} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac{2 \times 19}{2} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac{\cancel 2 \times 19}{\cancel 2} \\\\
a               &= 19
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number Jennie thought of}  &= 19 \\\\
\text{Number Freddie thought of} &= 30 - 19 \\\\ 
                                 &= 11
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$19, 11$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number Jennie thought of $= a$
<emptyline>
Let the number Freddie thought of $= b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a - b      &= 8 \\\\
a + b      &= 30
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
We have got $2$ variables and we have $2$ equations to solve them. Let's eliminate to reduce it to just one variable. 
<emptyline>
Let's add above two equation.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a - b + a + b   &= 8 + 30 \\\\
2a              &= 38 \\\\
a               &= \dfrac{38}{2} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac{2 \times 19}{2} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac{\cancel 2 \times 19}{\cancel 2} \\\\
a               &= 19
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number Jennie thought of}  &= 19 \\\\
\text{Number Freddie thought of} &= 30 - 19 \\\\ 
                                 &= 11
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$19, 11$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

George is $2$ years and $9$ months older than his sister Sophie.

George is $12$ years and $5$ months old.

How old is Sophie?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Sophie's age}  &= \text {George's age} - 2 \ \text {years} \ 9 \ \text {months} \\\\
                      &= 12 \ \text {years} \ 5 \ \text {months} - 2 \ \text {years} \ 9 \ \text {months} \\\\
                      &= 9 \ \text {years} \ 8 \ \text {months}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9 \ \text {years} \ 8 \ \text {months}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Sophie's age}  &= \text {George's age} - 2 \ \text {years} \ 9 \ \text {months} \\\\
                      &= 12 \ \text {years} \ 5 \ \text {months} - 2 \ \text {years} \ 9 \ \text {months} \\\\
                      &= 9 \ \text {years} \ 8 \ \text {months}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

A large barrel contains $3.25 \ \text{litres}$ of water.

Mel fills three $500 \ \text{ml}$ bottles from the barrel.

She uses all of the rest of the water to fill as many $150 \ \text{ml}$ cups as she can.

What is the largest number of cups which Mel could completely fill?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required
. Let's convert all the units into $\text {ml}$.

$ \text {As per the question,} $

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Largest number of cups}                  &= \dfrac {3.25 \ \text {litres} - 500 \ \text {ml} \times 3} {150 \ \text {ml}} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {3.25 \times 1000 \ \text {ml} - 500 \ \text {ml} \times 3} {150 \ \text {ml}} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {3250 - 500 \times 3} {150} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {3250 - 1500} {150} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {1750} {150} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {5 \times 35 \times 10} {5 \times 3 \times 10} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 35 \times \cancel 10} {\cancel 5 \times 3 \times \cancel 10} \\\\
                                                &= 11.67
\end{aligned}
$

$ \text{Largest number of cups}= 11$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$11$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_no_dot_note. Let's convert all the units into $\text {ml}$.
<emptyline>
$ \text {As per the question,} $
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Largest number of cups}                  &= \dfrac {3.25 \ \text {litres} - 500 \ \text {ml} \times 3} {150 \ \text {ml}} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {3.25 \times 1000 \ \text {ml} - 500 \ \text {ml} \times 3} {150 \ \text {ml}} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {3250 - 500 \times 3} {150} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {3250 - 1500} {150} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {1750} {150} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {5 \times 35 \times 10} {5 \times 3 \times 10} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 35 \times \cancel 10} {\cancel 5 \times 3 \times \cancel 10} \\\\
                                                &= 11.67
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$ \text{Largest number of cups}= 11$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$11$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

On the Gagalapos Islands, there lives an amazing animal called the Joak.

When it is born, the Joak has one head with a bristle, and one body segment with two pairs of bristles, as shown:

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-30-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-30-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-30-00.png")

At the start of each year, Joak grows one more identical body segment so that at the start of the second year it looks like this: 

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-30-01.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-30-01.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-30-01.png")

</div>

|       $2$       |         $2$        |         $9$        |

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many bristles will Joak have during its $10 \text{th}$ year? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of bristles}       &= 10 \times 4 + 1 \\\\
                                &= 40 + 1 \\\\
                                &= 41
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$41$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of bristles}       &= 10 \times 4 + 1 \\\\
                                &= 40 + 1 \\\\
                                &= 41
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$41$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

In which year will Joak have $65$ bristles?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the year $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 4 + 1          &= 65 \\\\
4a                      &= 65 - 1 \\\\
4a                      &= 64 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {64} {4} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {4 \times 16} {4} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 16} {\cancel 4} \\\\
a                       &= 16
\end{aligned}
$

The year in which Joak will have $65$ bristles $= 16$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$16$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the year $= a$
<emptyline>

a                       &= \dfrac {4 \times 16} {4} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 16} {\cancel 4} \\\\
a                       &= 16
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The year in which Joak will have $65$ bristles $= 16$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2:1:32</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Thomasina, Dixie and Harriet are playing a game of marbles.

They start with $20$ marbles each and they play three rounds.

In first round, Dixie wins $4$ marbles from Thomasina and loses $5$ to Harriet.

In the second round, Harriet wins $6$ marbles from Dixie and loses $2$ to Thomasina.

In the third round, Thomasina wins $3$ marbles from Harriet and loses $6$ to Dixie.

How many marbles does each girl have at the end of three round?

![missing table](/papers/missing_table.svg)


name       number of marbles
Thomasina
Dixie
Harriet

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of marbles left with Dixie after first round}       &= 20 + 4 - 5 \\\\
                                                                 &= 19 \\\\
\text{Number of marbles left with Thomasina after first round}   &= 20 - 4 \\\\
                                                                 &= 16 \\\\
\text{Number of marbles left with Harriet after first round}     &= 20 + 5 \\\\
                                                                 &= 25 \\\\
\text{Number of marbles left with Harriet after second round}     &= 25 + 6 - 2 \\\\
                                                                  &= 29 \\\\
\text{Number of marbles left with Dixie after second round}       &= 19 - 6 \\\\
                                                                  &= 13 \\\\
\text{Number of marbles left with Thomasina after second round}   &= 16 + 2 \\\\
                                                                  &= 18 \\\\
\text{Number of marbles left with Thomasina after third round}     &= 18 + 3 - 6 \\\\
                                                                   &= 15 \\\\
\text{Number of marbles left with Harriet after third round}       &= 29 - 3 \\\\
                                                                   &= 26 \\\\
\text{Number of marbles left with Dixie after third round}         &= 13 + 6 \\\\
                                                                   &= 19 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$15, 26, 19$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of marbles left with Dixie after first round}       &= 20 + 4 - 5 \\\\
                                                                 &= 19 \\\\
\text{Number of marbles left with Thomasina after first round}   &= 20 - 4 \\\\
                                                                 &= 16 \\\\
\text{Number of marbles left with Harriet after first round}     &= 20 + 5 \\\\
                                                                 &= 25 \\\\
\text{Number of marbles left with Harriet after second round}     &= 25 + 6 - 2 \\\\
                                                                  &= 29 \\\\
\text{Number of marbles left with Dixie after second round}       &= 19 - 6 \\\\
                                                                  &= 13 \\\\
\text{Number of marbles left with Thomasina after second round}   &= 16 + 2 \\\\
                                                                  &= 18 \\\\
\text{Number of marbles left with Thomasina after third round}     &= 18 + 3 - 6 \\\\
                                                                   &= 15 \\\\
\text{Number of marbles left with Harriet after third round}       &= 29 - 3 \\\\
                                                                   &= 26 \\\\
\text{Number of marbles left with Dixie after third round}         &= 13 + 6 \\\\
                                                                   &= 19 
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$15, 26, 19$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

In the diagrams below, the square and rectangle have the same perimeter. 

The square has an area of $64 \ \text{cm}^2$.

The length of the rectangle is three times the width of the rectangle.

Work out the length of the rectangle.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-35-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-35-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-35-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of square} = \text{side} \times \text{side}$


ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of rectangle} = 2 \times (\text{length} + \text{width})$


ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of square} = 4 \times \text{side}$


Let the side of the square $= s$

Let the width of the rectangle $= w$

Length of the rectangle $= 3w$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
s \times s              &= 64 \\\\
s^2                     &= 64 \\\\
s                       &= \sqrt {64} \\\\
s                       &= 8 \ \text {cm} \\\\
2 \times (3w + w)       &= 4 \times s \\\\
2 \times 4w             &= 4 \times 8 \\\\
8w                      &= 4 \times 8 \\\\
w                       &= \dfrac {4 \times 8} {8} \\\\
w                       &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel {8}} {\cancel {8}} \\\\
w                       &= 4 \ \text {cm} \\\\
\text {Length}          &= 3 \times w \\\\
                        &= 3 \times 4 \\\\
                        &= 12 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12 \ \text{cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_square_expanded
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_katex_formula_expanded_perimeter_rectangle
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_katex_formula_expanded_perimeter_square
<emptyline>
Let the side of the square $= s$
<emptyline>
Let the width of the rectangle $= w$
<emptyline>
Length of the rectangle $= 3w$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
s \times s              &= 64 \\\\
s^2                     &= 64 \\\\
s                       &= \sqrt {64} \\\\
s                       &= 8 \ \text {cm} \\\\
2 \times (3w + w)       &= 4 \times s \\\\
2 \times 4w             &= 4 \times 8 \\\\
8w                      &= 4 \times 8 \\\\
w                       &= \dfrac {4 \times 8} {8} \\\\
w                       &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel {8}} {\cancel {8}} \\\\
w                       &= 4 \ \text {cm} \\\\
\text {Length}          &= 3 \times w \\\\
                        &= 3 \times 4 \\\\
                        &= 12 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

In the following diagrams, the number underneath is calculated using a rule.

The rule is **"multiply the numbers in the white regions and then add the numbers in the grey regions."**

The total is written underneath the diagram.

For example:

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-36-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-36-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-36-00.png")

Fill in the dotted line to complete the following diagrams:

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-36-01.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-36-01.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-36-01.png")
![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-36-02.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-36-02.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-36-02.png")
![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-36-03.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-36-03.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-36-03.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

From first image,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 13 \times 3 + 7 + 8 \\\\
&= 39 + 15 \\\\
&= 54
\end{aligned}
$

From second image,

Let the missing number $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
12 \times 4 + a + 6 &= 55 \\\\
48 + a + 6 &= 55 \\\\
54 + a &= 55 \\\\
a &= 55 - 54 \\\\
a &= 1
\end{aligned}
$

From third image,

Let the first missing number $= a$

Let the second missing number $= b$

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times b + 9 + 8 &= 38 \\\\
ab + 17 &= 38 \\\\
ab &= 38 - 17 \\\\
ab &= 21 \\\\
a \times b &= 1 \times 21 \ \text{Or,}\ \\\\
a \times b &= 3 \times 7 \ \text{Or,}\ \\\\
a \times b &= 7 \times 3 \ \text{Or,}\ \\\\
a \times b &= 21 \times 1
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working placeholder'>

</div>
</div>

$
55 \\\\
1 \\\\
(1, 21) \ \text{Or,}\ (3, 7) \ \text{Or,}\ (7, 3) \ \text{Or,}\ (21, 1) \\\\
$

</div>
<div class='answer placeholder'>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

The diagram below shows the footpaths between villages.

Each village is represented by a letter.

For example, there are three different possible footpaths between villages $Q$ and $R$.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-37-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-37-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2/section-1-question-37-00.png")

Jane wants to walk from village $A$ and $B$ without travelling through any village twice on her route.

Work out how many different possible routes Jane could take?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using permutation,

If we follow $APB$ village, the number of possible route are $= 1$

If we follow $AQRSB$ village, the number of possible route are $= 2 \times 3 = 6$

If we follow $ATUVB$ village, the number of possible route are $= 2 \times 3 \times 2 = 12$

So, total possible routes $= 1 + 6 + 12 = 19$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$19$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using permutation,
<emptyline>
If we follow $APB$ village, the number of possible route are $= 1$
<emptyline>
If we follow $AQRSB$ village, the number of possible route are $= 2 \times 3 = 6$
<emptyline>
If we follow $ATUVB$ village, the number of possible route are $= 2 \times 3 \times 2 = 12$
<emptyline>
So, total possible routes $= 1 + 6 + 12 = 19$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$19$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>


</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_pr rag_prrl question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>6CB0F5E6-F7EC-4E39-816A-4384AF737221</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2013--group-2:1:38</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_pr</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prrl</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>

</li>
<li>
tinfer
</li>
</ul>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Magda and her friends go to the cinema.

They buy $1$ large tub of popcorn, $4$ fizzy drinks, and $10$ packets of crisps.

The bill comes to $\pounds 10.89$

Lena and her friends go to the cinema.

They buy $1$ large tub of popcorn, $3$ fizzy drinks, and $7$ packets of crisps.

The bill comes to $\pounds 8.64$

How much would it cost to buy $1$ large tub of popcorn, $1$ fizzy drink and $1$ packet of crisps?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the cost of popcorn $= p$

Let the cost of fizzy drinks $= d$

Let the cost of crisps $= c$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
p + 4d + 10c   &= \pounds 10.89 \\\\
p + 3d + 7c    &= \pounds 9.64 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

We will use elimination method to find the value of 1 large tub of popcorn, 1 fizzy drink and 1 packet of crisps.

First Subtract second equation from first

$
\begin{aligned}
p + 4d + 10c - (p + 3d + 7c)  &= 10.89 - 8.64 \\\\
p + 4d + 10c - p - 3d - 7c    &= 2.25 \\\\
d + 3c                        &= 2.25 \\\\                     
\end{aligned}
$

Now multiply the above equation by 3 and then subtract it from the first equation.

$
\begin{aligned}
3 \times (d + 3c)        &= 3\times 2.25 \\\\ 
3d + 9c                  &= 6.75 \\\\
p + 4d + 10c - (3d + 9c) &= 10.89 - 6.75 \\\\
p + d + c                &= 4.14
\end{aligned}
$

Cost of 1 large tub of popcorn, 11 fizzy drink and 11 packet of crisps $= 4.14$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4.14$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the cost of popcorn $= p$
<emptyline>
Let the cost of fizzy drinks $= d$
<emptyline>
Let the cost of crisps $= c$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
p + 4d + 10c   &= \pounds 10.89 \\\\
p + 3d + 7c    &= \pounds 9.64 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
We will use elimination method to find the value of 1 large tub of popcorn, 1 fizzy drink and 1 packet of crisps.
<emptyline>
First Subtract second equation from first
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
p + 4d + 10c - (p + 3d + 7c)  &= 10.89 - 8.64 \\\\
p + 4d + 10c - p - 3d - 7c    &= 2.25 \\\\
d + 3c                        &= 2.25 \\\\                     
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Now multiply the above equation by 3 and then subtract it from the first equation.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
3 \times (d + 3c)        &= 3\times 2.25 \\\\ 
3d + 9c                  &= 6.75 \\\\
p + 4d + 10c - (3d + 9c) &= 10.89 - 6.75 \\\\
p + d + c                &= 4.14
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Cost of 1 large tub of popcorn, 11 fizzy drink and 11 packet of crisps $= 4.14$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4.14$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
